# 1\. 「ライブ前のひと時」 — Mei as Moon Tempest’s tension-breaker

**Importance tier: Core.**

This is one of the best cards for understanding Mei’s role inside Moon Tempest.

Before a summer festival live, the group is unusually tense. The new song has a different atmosphere from previous Moon Tempest material, so the members are worried about fan reception. Mei sees the problem clearly: everyone’s faces are stiff, conversation has decreased, and they are thinking too much right before the stage. Her solution is to start water play, beginning with Kotono and Suzu, then dragging the whole group into it. card\_mei\_010\_st-card-mei-05-miz…

This is classic Mei: emotionally accurate, logistically absurd, socially effective.

The card does not present her as oblivious to the stakes. She understands why everyone is nervous. But her instinct is that after doing all the preparation they can, further rumination is useless. What the group needs before performance is not more analysis, but restored aliveness.

That gives Mei a specific unit function:

**Mei returns Moon Tempest to the body.**

Kotono can overthink. Nagisa can narrativize. Saki can discipline. Suzu can dramatize. Mei makes them splash water, laugh, loosen their faces, and remember that a live is something to enjoy.

The second and third sections add an important mature layer. After the successful live, Mei invites the manager to a seaside donut outing. At first this looks like standard Mei food comedy, but she explains that she wanted to talk slowly with him, like they used to do at the sea back in Hoshimi. Tokyo success has reduced those quiet intervals, and Mei notices. card\_mei\_010\_st-card-mei-05-miz…

Then, on the beach, she reflects on the song’s creation. The important part was not only that they made a good song; the process of talking, clashing, struggling, and playing deepened the group’s bonds. This is unusually reflective for Mei, and it becomes a stronger ambition statement: she wants to make more people smile, work hard with the five of them, and someday reach the top of the idol world with Moon Tempest. card\_mei\_010\_st-card-mei-05-miz…

The “恋” line is also revealing. Mei says her stronger will may come from being in love — with Moon Tempest, and then pointedly says 月ストに「も」, “with Moon Tempest too.” The ambiguity is playful and manager-facing, but the deeper point is that Mei’s emotional vocabulary remains expansive. Love is not neatly divided into romance, group loyalty, fan joy, food, and play. For Mei, all of these are forms of energizing attachment.

**What it adds:**
Mei is not merely Moon Tempest’s bright one. She is the member who knows how to break the spell of anxiety. She turns pressure back into play, and play back into performance strength.

---

# 2\. 「セクシーなアレンジ」 — Mei as public-contact genius and spontaneous helper

**Importance tier: Important to core.**

This card begins with costume comedy: Mei is reviewing summer festival outfits and wants to make them cuter and easier to move in, but her proposed “sexy arrangement” becomes too revealing. This matters more than it first appears because it shows Mei’s attitude toward her own body and idol image. Unlike Nagisa, who becomes self-conscious about visibility, Mei is quite comfortable with being seen if the outfit feels cute and fun. She does not seem to process “sexy” as dangerous or heavily adult-coded; she treats it as another form of playful cuteness. card\_mei\_011\_st-card-mei-05-miz…

But the real core of the card is the beach-house sequence. Mei notices an elderly man running a beach food stand while visibly struggling with back pain. Later, despite being told not to go outside in costume before the live, she slips out and starts helping him call customers, recommend food, serve people, and build a crowd. card\_mei\_011\_st-card-mei-05-miz…

This is an excellent example of Mei’s care pattern. She does not sit and worry. She helps physically and immediately.

The manager understands that she had been worried about the old man from the beginning, and chooses not to scold her harshly. During the live, the old man and his customers show up, and Mei openly promotes the shop from the stage. The audience atmosphere becomes warmer, and the manager frames that warmth as Mei’s personality. card\_mei\_011\_st-card-mei-05-miz…

The final section gives the emotional reason: the beach-house owner reminded Mei of a dagashi shop grandfather near her childhood home in Hoshimi City. As a child, she went there every day and talked with him. So when she saw an elderly man waiting alone for customers, she could not ignore him. card\_mei\_011\_st-card-mei-05-miz…

This is crucial because it reveals that Mei’s impulsive kindness often has a memory underneath. She is not simply distractible; she is porous to resemblance. A stranger can call up an old local relationship, and Mei acts as though the stranger is already part of her care-world.

The old man says he had planned to close after that year, but Mei’s help and live gave him energy to keep going a little longer. This is one of the cleanest examples of Mei’s idol effect outside formal stage performance: she extends someone’s will to continue. card\_mei\_011\_st-card-mei-05-miz…

**What it adds:**
Mei’s idol power is not confined to singing and dancing. Her public charisma is immediate, practical, and local: she can turn a food stand into a crowd, a crowd into an audience, and an elderly man’s fatigue into one more year of trying.

---

# 3\. 「すっごい嬉しいニュース」 — Mei/Suzu, Mana memory, and the shape of affection

**Importance tier: Core for Mei/Suzu and Mei/Mana.**

This is the most important Mei/Suzu card in this batch.

The setup is the aftermath of a unit battle where Mei and Suzu performed together and were praised by viewers. Mei is happy to be recognized, but also a little lonely that the temporary unit is over. Then the manager tells her the song will be officially released because the performance was so well received. Mei’s joy explodes, and her first instinct is to run to Suzu. card\_mei\_012\_st-card-mei-05-pai…

That establishes Mei’s relational priority: success is not complete until shared with the person who made it meaningful.

The second section is one of the strongest Mana-memory passages for Mei. Mei goes shopping with the manager after Suzu’s plans fall through, and she remembers coming to the same mall with Mana. Her memories are warm, silly, and highly specific: looking at dogs and deciding which one looked like the manager, talking about ghost-house dates, hearing that Mana was scared of haunted houses despite being a ghost, and hearing about the time the manager seriously scolded Mana after she collapsed from overwork. card\_mei\_012\_st-card-mei-05-pai…

The key line is that remembering Mana and the manager made Mei realize she needed to properly confront Suzu. She wanted to collide seriously with Suzu the way Mana and the manager had. This is a major refinement of Mei’s Mana relationship.

For Kotono, Mana is the dead sister whose memory must be answered.
For Suzu, Mana is the idol ideal to worship and inherit.
For Mei, Mana is a friend whose lived stories become relational instruction.

Mei does not only remember Mana as a legendary idol. She remembers Mana as someone who went shopping, teased the manager, got embarrassed, overworked, and was scolded by someone who cared. That lets Mana remain socially alive in Mei’s memory.

The final section, with the unit battle afterparty and jacket shoot, further defines Mei/Suzu. Mei imitates Rei, Haruko, and Suzu badly but lovingly, then later describes Suzu as dog-like: cute, straightforward, affectionate, sometimes reliable, and cute again. She says she loves Suzu directly. card\_mei\_012\_st-card-mei-05-pai…

The “manager is not father” ending is also notable. Mei says it is nice that the manager has always been beside them, but “father” feels wrong. She starts to define what he is instead, then refuses to say it. This is not enough to declare a clean romantic line, but it does show that Mei does not experience him only as a paternal caretaker. As with her earlier romance-game material, she has feelings of closeness that exceed the simple categories she has available. card\_mei\_012\_st-card-mei-05-pai…

**What it adds:**
Mei/Suzu is a genuine paired-growth relationship, not just a gag duo. Mei’s memories of Mana help her learn to confront Suzu sincerely, and Suzu becomes one of the people through whom Mei’s joy gains focus and depth.

---

# 4\. 「今の芽衣とは違う芽衣」 — supernatural comedy as Mei’s natural habitat

**Importance tier: Important texture; core for Mei’s “porous reality” register.**

This card is bizarre, but it is very valuable because it intensifies something already established about Mei: she is the character most comfortable with impossible things.

The setup links to a Haruhi crossover premise. Mei insists that the earlier SOS Brigade-like experience was not a dream. She remembers a club whose purpose was to make the world exciting, a future girl, supernatural people, and a live performance. The manager is skeptical, but he already knows Mei’s family has a connection to seeing ghosts, so the boundary between dream, supernatural incident, and comedy is not fully secure around her. card\_mei\_007\_st-card-mei-05-hru…

Then future Meis appear. One says she came from the future because the manager disappeared before they could buy donuts. Another arrives to prevent him from eating donuts that will give him food poisoning. More future Meis arrive with increasingly absurd timelines: the manager becoming a dark fixer, anti-donut aliens, and so on. The jokes escalate into “タイムパイナップル,” Mei’s mangled version of “time paradox.” card\_mei\_007\_st-card-mei-05-hru…

On the surface, this is pure crossover farce. But as Mei characterization, it does three things.

First, it literalizes Mei’s multiplicity. Different future Meis all share the same basic instincts: donuts, emotional concern, panic over the manager, chaotic logic, and immediate action. Even across timelines, Mei remains Mei.

Second, it shows that her mind is compatible with absurd metaphysics because she does not over-police reality. She accepts ghosts, future people, forbidden information, and strange phenomena through the same open channel by which she accepts cats, donuts, and new friends.

Third, it reinforces that Mei’s concern for the manager is emotionally genuine even when expressed through nonsense. The future Meis come because they were sad, worried, or wanted to prevent harm. The emotional motive is simple; the plot mechanism is insane. That is Mei.

**What it adds:**
Mei’s supernatural/absurd register is not random flavor. It suits her because she is already a boundary-loose character: between play and work, food and philosophy, ghosts and everyday life, comedy and concern, present feeling and future possibility.

---

# 5\. 「芽衣のポテンシャル」 — donuts as motivation, metaphor, and idol concept

**Importance tier: Core baseline.**

This is an early story, set before the NEXT VENUS preliminaries, and it is one of the most useful baseline Mei cards.

The first section shows the classic “Mei problem”: she is on the verge of failing schoolwork, wants the manager to teach her, immediately falls asleep, and needs an external reward to focus. But the punchline is not that she is stupid. Once donuts are offered as motivation — one donut for avoiding red marks, all-you-can-eat donuts for above-average scores — she suddenly shows serious concentration and produces high scores. card\_mei\_008\_st-card-mei-05-ido…

This is important. Mei’s issue is not lack of capacity. It is activation. When interest, reward, or emotional energy connects to the task, her potential appears dramatically.

The donut shop section then converts a gag into philosophy. Mei gives 108 reasons she loves donuts. Most are food comedy, but the final one is surprisingly meaningful: donuts are rings, and rings feel like connection. She compares them to the circle the group forms before a live. From this, she invents “ドーナツ系アイドル” — a donut-type idol — whose singing makes the audience join hands and become a circle like a donut. card\_mei\_008\_st-card-mei-05-ido…

This is the exact shape of Mei’s imagination: concrete object → sensory enjoyment → social metaphor → performance concept.

The final gravure section with cats and flowers shows her natural camera strength. She thinks she is going to play with cats, but that very sincerity makes her smile natural and photogenic. The adults get pulled into her pace, and even the manager is dragged into a photo. The photographer praises the natural smile, confirming that Mei’s public appeal works best when she is not trying to manufacture image. card\_mei\_008\_st-card-mei-05-ido…

**What it adds:**
Mei’s “donut” motif is not merely appetite. It is her model of idolhood: make people form a circle, share something sweet, and turn performance into togetherness.

---

# 6\. 「プールを遊び尽くすよ～！！」 — Mei as summer sun, and the discovery of care through panic

**Importance tier: Core for emotional seriousness beneath play.**

This card starts as pure Mei summer energy. She treats every season as a source of vitality: summer sun, spring cherry blossoms, autumn leaves, winter snow. This is a major personality statement. Mei does not only love summer; she converts seasonal change itself into energy. card\_mei\_009\_st-card-mei-05-mik…

Her reason for wanting a pool job is also relational. She regrets not getting to play much with the manager during the earlier sea outing, so she proposes that Moon Tempest get a pool job so the manager can come along as staff. When the Miku collaboration song gets an MV shoot at a pool, she is thrilled not only because the song is being released, but because he remembered the promise and adjusted the schedule with her happiness in mind. card\_mei\_009\_st-card-mei-05-mik…

The middle section repeats Mei’s limit problem. She is so excited she cannot sleep, then plays during break instead of resting, dragging the exhausted manager through slides and attractions. Then the two get trapped in a hot storeroom because she misunderstood the door. When the manager lies down and falls asleep, Mei thinks he has collapsed from heatstroke and panics. card\_mei\_009\_st-card-mei-05-mik…

Her anger afterward is one of the most emotionally serious Mei moments so far. She calls him an idiot, scolds him for overworking and not sleeping, and says she was truly worried. Then, almost immediately, she blames herself for dragging him around when he was tired. card\_mei\_009\_st-card-mei-05-mik…

This is the important corrective to the idea that Mei is only carefree. She can be carefree because she assumes people are okay. When she thinks someone she cares about may be seriously hurt, her emotional world snaps into focus.

The resolution is beautiful: she says she was not excited simply because she wanted to play in the pool. She was excited because she wanted to play with him. So rather than continue chasing attractions alone, she chooses to sit and talk with cold drinks. card\_mei\_009\_st-card-mei-05-mik…

That is a major growth beat. Mei does not abandon fun, but she learns to relocate fun from activity to companionship.

The final MV shoot frames her visually as “水面を照らす夏の太陽” — the summer sun lighting the water’s surface. This is one of the most explicit visual/thematic labels for Mei: she is not Moon Tempest’s moonlight in the same register as Kotono. She is summer sunlight inside the unit, brightening the water, making everything glitter. card\_mei\_009\_st-card-mei-05-mik…

**What it adds:**
Mei’s joy matures when she realizes that the person matters more than the activity. She remains energetic, but the card gives her a serious care reflex and a sun-image that will be important for future visual/character analysis.

---

# Cross-card synthesis: Mei after this batch

## 1\. Mei’s role in Moon Tempest is emotional activation

Mei’s job is not just to be funny. She activates others.

She loosens Moon Tempest before a risky new song.
She warms a summer-festival audience through the beach-house owner.
She turns Suzu partnership into a release-worthy unit song.
She draws the manager into rest, conversation, and play.
She makes children, cats, fans, staff, and strangers respond.
She reframes donuts as a circle of connection.

Her idolhood is fundamentally **participatory**. She does not shine from a distance. She pulls people into the event.

## 2\. Mei is “sun” within the “moon” unit

Moon Tempest is structured around moonlight, darkness, reflection, and paths through uncertainty. Mei complicates that palette. She is not lunar in the Kotono sense. She is more like a small internal sun: seasonally bright, impulsive, direct, bodily, warming, and visually associated here with water, summer, and glittering reflected light.

That does not make her incompatible with Moon Tempest. It makes her necessary.

A moonlit unit without Mei could become too solemn, too stiff, too proud, or too inward. Mei keeps Moon Tempest alive in the body.

## 3\. Mei’s food motifs are serious

Donuts are Mei’s most important recurring object, and this batch proves they should not be dismissed as a gag.

Donuts are:

-   motivation;

-   reward;

-   sensory joy;

-   craft knowledge;

-   fan hospitality;

-   birthday memory;

-   a metaphor for circles;

-   a way to invite others;

-   a symbol of togetherness;

-   and a medium through which Mei thinks about idolhood.


The “donut-type idol” idea sounds silly, but it is actually one of Mei’s clearest self-definitions: an idol whose performance forms a ring of connection.

## 4\. Mei’s “love” remains expansive and ambiguous

This batch continues the earlier romance-game pattern. Mei uses “date,” “love,” and closeness language easily, sometimes toward the manager, sometimes toward Moon Tempest, sometimes toward Suzu, sometimes toward general shared fun.

That does not mean every instance is literal romance. In Mei’s emotional grammar, “suki” and “koi” are broad, embodied, and experience-based. She often realizes the intimacy of what she is saying only after saying it.

This makes her one of the more interesting characters for analyzing pre-romantic or undifferentiated affection: she has intense attachment, but her categories lag behind her feelings.

## 5\. Mei and Mana are connected through lived memory

Mana’s presence in Mei’s arc is distinctive. Mei does not treat Mana only as sacred legend. She remembers time spent together: shopping, dogs, haunted houses, the manager, overwork, embarrassment, ordinary play.

This is important because Mei preserves a version of Mana that is not mythic. Mei’s Mana is funny, active, teasing, tired, scared of haunted houses, and relationally alive.

That makes Mei a quiet anti-shrine character. She keeps Mana human.

## 6\. Mei’s growth problem is pacing, not seriousness

The repeated Mei issue is that she overdoes things:

-   trains through injury;

-   cannot sleep from excitement;

-   plays during rest time;

-   drags tired adults around;

-   rushes off before thinking;

-   forgets practical details;

-   assumes fun can continue indefinitely.


But this is not because she lacks seriousness. It is because joy feels like infinite fuel to her.

Her growth is learning that bodies, promises, and people have limits — and that fun can become deeper when it slows down.

---

# Revised importance tiers

## Core

**ライブ前のひと時** — Mei as tension-breaker, creative-process reflector, and Moon Tempest ambition speaker; strong statement of “I want to reach the top with the five of us.” card\_mei\_010\_st-card-mei-05-miz…

**すっごい嬉しいニュース** — Core Mei/Suzu and Mei/Mana card; defines the paired unit, Mana as lived memory, and Mei’s use of Mana/manager memories to confront Suzu sincerely. card\_mei\_012\_st-card-mei-05-pai…

**芽衣のポテンシャル** — Core early baseline; establishes activation-through-interest, donut philosophy, natural smile, cat/flower photogenic appeal, and “donut-type idol” as connection metaphor. card\_mei\_008\_st-card-mei-05-ido…

**プールを遊び尽くすよ～！！** — Core for Mei’s care seriousness and visual/thematic sun identity; “fun because with you,” not merely “fun because pool.” card\_mei\_009\_st-card-mei-05-mik…

## Important

**セクシーなアレンジ** — Important for Mei’s body/image comfort, spontaneous help, local-childhood compassion, and public-contact charisma. card\_mei\_011\_st-card-mei-05-miz…

**今の芽衣とは違う芽衣** — Important for Mei’s supernatural/comic porousness and Haruhi-style reality logic; less central emotionally, but highly useful for voice and genre texture. card\_mei\_007\_st-card-mei-05-hru…

---

# Updated Mei thesis after this batch

**Hayasaka Mei is Moon Tempest’s kinetic sun: a girl whose joy becomes motion, whose motion becomes contact, and whose contact turns people into a circle. Her apparent airheadedness conceals a powerful idol instinct: she knows when people need to laugh, move, eat, play, be noticed, or be pulled back into the present. She does not process feeling through solemn analysis; she processes it through action — splashing water before a live, helping an old man’s beach stand, chasing cats, sharing donuts, dragging someone to the pool, or telling Suzu she loves her. Her weakness is that joy can make her ignore limits, but her growth is learning that fun is not only speed and activity. It is also companionship, memory, care, and the promise to meet again next year.**

**Assistant — Turn 46**

This batch is a major refinement of Mei. The earlier material established her as **Moon Tempest’s kinetic joy / internal summer sun**: the girl who turns feeling into motion, pulls people into circles, and makes smiles contagious. These five cards add a sharper second layer:

**Mei is beginning to discover “seriousness” without losing Mei-ness.**
Not seriousness as solemnity, restraint, or Kotono-like discipline, but seriousness as **本気 / honki** — genuine full-force commitment. She wants to help Rei. She wants to support Kotono’s birthday. She wants to give her parents something. She wants to understand what her own “本命” feeling means. She wants to stand in strange, risky, unconventional performance spaces and still be an idol.

The strongest new thesis is:

> **Mei’s growth is not from childishness into maturity, but from undifferentiated joy into chosen sincerity. She remains impulsive, absurd, food-driven, supernatural-adjacent, and physically chaotic, but she increasingly asks: what feeling am I putting into this action, and who do I want it to reach?**

---

# Overall contribution to Mei

This batch deepens Mei in five areas.

First, it shows that Mei’s “fun-first” worldview can become **artistic courage**. In the underground idol arena card, she is drawn to an extreme, unofficial performance world not because she wants cheap danger, but because she recognizes conviction, spectacle, and idols who refuse to abandon their style even after being pushed outside the official system. Her taste for “ばりばりどっかーーん” is ridiculous, but it also shows that Mei is receptive to idol energy wherever it appears. card\_mei\_015\_st-card-mei-05-wdn…

Second, it gives Mei one of her clearest forms of **relational activism** through Rei. She is worried that Rei’s dance has become too tense and driven by parental pressure, so she acts — clumsily, intrusively, but sincerely — to get Rei’s father to witness Rei’s dancing. This card is crucial because Mei does not merely say “I want Rei to be happy.” She turns that desire into concrete action. card\_mei\_013\_st-card-mei-05-roc…

Third, it clarifies Mei’s family orientation. The wedding card and Christmas card both show that Mei loves her parents openly and wants to give back to them. She wants to show her father her wedding-dress photo because he joked that seeing his daughter as a bride would cheer him up, and she later wants to win the Atami trip for both parents. Her filial affection is direct, uncomplicated, and deeply important. card\_mei\_016\_st-card-mei-05-wed… card\_mei\_017\_st-card-mei-05-xma…

Fourth, this batch gives Mei’s manager relationship much stronger ambiguity. Earlier, Mei’s feelings could be read mostly as broad affectionate attachment. Here, the wedding and Valentine cards explicitly push into romantic-adjacent territory: tuxedo/wedding photos, “ずっとずっと、一緒にいようね,” “本命チョコ,” “ずっと芽衣を見てて,” and “芽衣も{user}さんの気持ちちゃんと知りたい.” Yet Mei still processes these feelings in her own category system, not standard adult romance categories. card\_mei\_016\_st-card-mei-05-wed… card\_mei\_014\_st-card-mei-05-vln…

Fifth, this batch confirms that Mei’s truthfulness is central. She struggles to lie to Kotono during the Christmas surprise because Kotono is her friend; she can accept a “secret” because it feels special and shared, but not a lie because that violates her straightness. On stage, the manager observes that Mei’s words reach fans because there is no lie in them. card\_mei\_017\_st-card-mei-05-xma…

---

# 1\. 「どっかーーーーん」 — Mei and the underground idol arena

**Importance tier: Important to core.**

This is one of Mei’s strangest cards, but it is important because it tests her idol instincts outside the safe, official Hoshimi/VENUS environment.

The setup is pure Mei chaos: late-night screaming, suspicious sunglasses, secret meetings with Rei, Aoi, and Chisa, and phrases like “ブラッディドロドロドーナツ.” But the explanation reveals that she is writing lyrics for a new underground-idol battle performance with Rei and Aoi. The venue is the “アイドル地下闘技場,” an unofficial idol battle arena where non-VENUS idols perform in extreme, theatrical live battles. card\_mei\_015\_st-card-mei-05-wdn…

The crucial thing is that Mei does not react with institutional snobbery. She is fascinated. She sees an unofficial, dangerous-looking, excessive space and responds: this has energy. This is stimulating. There is something here that normal idol stages may not provide.

Her admiration for the group “鎖鎌” is revealing. They were apparently removed from VENUS after repeatedly using explosion-style stage gimmicks, yet Mei admires that they continued as “野良アイドル” while holding onto their convictions. This is very Mei: she is attracted to people who keep performing with full bodily commitment, even if the form is absurd. card\_mei\_015\_st-card-mei-05-wdn…

The middle section, where the manager is pulled into participating, is comic but analytically valuable. Mei becomes the “senpai” who supports the manager’s idol training. When he breaks down over his lack of idol talent, she reassures him that his love of idols and genuine effort are reaching her. This reverses their usual dynamic: Mei is not only the childlike idol being managed; she can also recognize and encourage someone else’s struggling performance. card\_mei\_015\_st-card-mei-05-wdn…

The final live is the most important part. Faced with bizarre veteran opponents and an extreme venue, Mei does not shrink. She performs for the people in front of her, tries to energize even the elderly rival idols, and shines in a place where the manager initially feels overwhelmed. He thinks: wherever she is, Mei sings and dances openly for the people watching — she really is an idol. card\_mei\_015\_st-card-mei-05-wdn…

**What it adds:**
Mei’s idolhood is portable. She can remain an idol even in strange, unofficial, chaotic, low-prestige spaces. She is not dependent on stage purity. If there are people watching and feelings moving, Mei can turn it into performance.

---

# 2\. 「水が垂れてるいい男？」 — wedding, family, and adult-adjacent emotional ambiguity

**Importance tier: Core for family and manager relationship.**

This card begins with a very grounded family situation: Mei’s father is hospitalized with a strained back after trying to lift a box of books. It is comic, but it immediately shows Mei’s family intimacy. She teases her father as clumsy, but she wants to visit quickly and worries about him. card\_mei\_016\_st-card-mei-05-wed…

The emotional core arrives when Mei explains why she wants to wear a wedding dress. Her father jokingly said that seeing his cute daughter as a bride would cheer him up, but Mei noticed that he seemed genuinely lonely and smaller than usual. Because she has been busy and unable to return home often, she wants to do something filial for him. This makes the wedding concept not merely romantic fanservice; it is rooted in parent-child love. card\_mei\_016\_st-card-mei-05-wed…

At the same time, the card strongly plays with romance. Mei notices the manager’s wet hair after a sudden rain, gets a “dokidoki” feeling, and tries to describe him with the mangled phrase “水が垂れてるいい男.” She then wants to dry his hair herself. This is classic Mei: physical care, romantic-adjacent reaction, wrong phrase, immediate action. card\_mei\_016\_st-card-mei-05-wed…

The dress-selection section leans even harder into romcom territory. Mei teases the manager, pretends Kotono has appeared, and eventually arranges for him to wear a tuxedo during the shoot. The stated reason is that a wedding photo with two people is more fun and useful as reference, but the effect is obviously couple-coded. card\_mei\_016\_st-card-mei-05-wed…

The third section is one of Mei’s most revealing emotional passages. In the wedding setting, she reflects that marriage is amazing because it means living with someone for decades. If she were to marry, she would want someone with whom she can have fun at home or outside, no matter what they are doing. She immediately says she feels that way with the manager — then broadens it to Moon Tempest, family, and cats. That expansion is important: Mei’s emotional categories are still inclusive and undifferentiated, but the manager is specifically named first in a marriage-coded context. card\_mei\_016\_st-card-mei-05-wed…

Her later line is even stronger: she had so much fun choosing the dress with him that she wished the time would continue forever, and she says she will keep working hard as an idol so she can spend even more fun time with him. Then: “これからも、ずっとずっと、一緒にいようね.” The manager notices that she can make a serious, gentle, beautiful expression. card\_mei\_016\_st-card-mei-05-wed…

**What it adds:**
Mei’s affectionate ambiguity becomes much stronger. She still does not cleanly separate romance from family, group, pets, fun, and gratitude, but this card shows her capable of ceremonial seriousness and future-oriented intimacy. She may not fully know what category her feelings belong to, but she knows she wants “more time together.”

---

# 3\. 「街中すっかりクリスマス」 — truthfulness, Kotono’s birthday, and happiness without possession

**Importance tier: Core.**

This is one of Mei’s best cards for defining her moral simplicity.

The setup is Moon Tempest preparing a Christmas live that will secretly celebrate Kotono’s birthday. Mei frames the project not as “暗躍” — secret plotting — but as becoming Kotono’s Santa. That word choice matters. Mei does not like the idea that she is doing something shady; she is doing something good, so she wants the language to feel good too. card\_mei\_017\_st-card-mei-05-xma…

Her imagination is immediately over-budget and physically impossible: giant trees sprouting during the song, real reindeer dancing with the group, the manager arriving in a luxury car as Santa. This is Mei’s usual scale problem. Her heart goes straight to spectacle before reality catches up. But once brought back to earth, she shifts to a homemade birthday/Christmas cake. card\_mei\_017\_st-card-mei-05-xma…

The middle section is extremely useful for Mei’s ethics. Kotono starts messaging, and Mei cannot lie convincingly. She begins with absurdly formal seasonal-letter language, panics, tries to be “normal,” and then admits she does not want to lie to Kotono because Kotono is her friend. The eventual compromise — being in the dorm and saying there is nothing to worry about — lets Mei preserve the surprise without directly lying. card\_mei\_017\_st-card-mei-05-xma…

Her distinction between lie and secret is crucial: **嘘は嫌だけど、秘密ならいいかも**. She dislikes lying because it breaks straight trust, but a shared secret can feel special because it exists between close people. This captures Mei beautifully. She is not naïve because she has no concept of concealment; she is naïve because she wants concealment to remain affectionate rather than deceptive. card\_mei\_017\_st-card-mei-05-xma…

The live then shows Mei’s fan-facing power. She tells the audience Moon Tempest will be everyone’s Santa, asks fans for their own “present” in the form of huge support, and creates strong unity. The manager explicitly thinks Mei’s words reach fans because they contain no lies. That is one of the most important Mei statements in all the card material. card\_mei\_017\_st-card-mei-05-xma…

The final gift sequence is even more revealing. Mei gives the manager a hand mixer because he struggled while helping make the cake. It is a practical gift, but also a future-oriented one: now he can make cakes when celebrating Mei someday, and they can make cakes together again. When he tries to get her a Christmas present in return, she chooses another lottery attempt. They win the Atami trip she wanted for her parents. She defines the outcome as everyone happy: her parents get the trip, and she feels happy because the manager’s participation made it “his” gift to her. Even though nothing remains materially in Mei’s hands, she calls it happiness. card\_mei\_017\_st-card-mei-05-xma…

**What it adds:**
Mei’s generosity is not transactional. She can receive happiness through the act of giving, through shared participation, and through the feeling that someone helped her make someone else happy.

---

# 4\. 「楽しく踊ってる方がいい」 — Rei, family approval, and Mei’s action-based care

**Importance tier: Core for Mei/Rei and Mei’s relational intelligence.**

This card is one of the best examples of Mei caring about another idol’s inner state.

After a limited unit with Rei is decided, Mei becomes preoccupied with Rei’s relationship with her father. She notices that Rei’s dance has become more intense and dangerous, almost like old LizNoir during the Grand Prix era. Mei’s diagnosis is simple but accurate: Rei’s dancing is coolest when she is having fun, not when she is dancing under pressure. card\_mei\_013\_st-card-mei-05-roc…

This is very Mei. She may not understand complex family conflict intellectually, but she can read the difference between joyful movement and strained movement. Her concern is physical and emotional: Rei is not smiling; therefore something is wrong.

Her first attempt to help is clumsy. She talks at Rei about how important fathers and mothers are, which only confuses Rei. Then, while walking with the manager, she sees a little girl watching a Sunny Peace video. The girl freezes in awe during Rei’s dance solo, and Mei realizes that even a young child can understand Rei’s dance. This gives her the idea: Rei’s father needs to see Rei dance. card\_mei\_013\_st-card-mei-05-roc…

The method is reckless: Mei and Suzu go to Rei’s family home and ask her father to attend the live. It is the kind of thing Saki would scold, and the story acknowledges that they were indeed scolded. But the important point is that Mei’s action changes the situation. Rei’s father appears, Rei regains force and sparkle, and the live succeeds. card\_mei\_013\_st-card-mei-05-roc…

Afterward, Mei is not proud in a self-congratulatory way. She is happy because Rei is praised, because Rei’s face softened, and because dancing with Rei made Mei herself better. She describes a synergistic effect: Rei pulls her forward, lets her do difficult choreography, and makes her want to know Rei even more. card\_mei\_013\_st-card-mei-05-roc…

The final insight is very important: when you form a unit with someone, you think about them differently, learn unknown things, and come to love them more. This is a major Mei philosophy statement. Pair units are not merely musical formats; for Mei, they are engines of relational knowledge. card\_mei\_013\_st-card-mei-05-roc…

**What it adds:**
Mei’s care is unsophisticated but effective because it is embodied. She sees when someone’s dance loses joy, and she tries to restore the conditions under which that person can dance happily.

---

# 5\. 「面白いよね、『本命チョコ』」 — Mei’s honmei feeling and full-force sincerity

**Importance tier: Core.**

This is one of Mei’s most important emotional-development cards.

The first section begins with Mei researching the manager’s preferences for Valentine’s chocolate: sweet or bitter, heart or cat shape. The key reveal is that she is making **本命チョコ / honmei choko**, usually “chocolate for one’s real romantic interest.” She says it is special chocolate and that she will work hard on it. The manager panics, thinking she may have fallen in love with someone and might be moving away from idolhood. card\_mei\_014\_st-card-mei-05-vln…

The middle section reframes everything through the recent Moon Tempest center battle. Mei says she lost and was genuinely frustrated. She accepted the result, but her chest tightened with the feeling that she wanted to win and wanted to become center. Importantly, she is happy to recognize that feeling because it proves she was serious. So she decides to make the chocolate seriously too: no regret, full force, because that is more fun and will make the recipient happier. card\_mei\_014\_st-card-mei-05-vln…

This is a major maturation beat.

Earlier Mei’s “seriousness” often looked like excitement. Here she explicitly identifies frustration, desire to win, and the pleasure of knowing she was truly committed. She is learning that negative feelings — 悔しい, not wanting to lose — can also be signs of love and ambition.

The final section gives the emotional payoff. Mei gives the chocolate to the manager. When he asks if this should really be honmei rather than obligation/giri chocolate, Mei says she asked classmates and thought about it carefully. Giri chocolate is “thank you,” and while gratitude is part of her feeling, it is not all of it. card\_mei\_014\_st-card-mei-05-vln…

Then she gives perhaps the clearest statement of her affectionate ambiguity: she does not know exactly what feelings are inside because there are too many. “Keep watching me,” “please continue from now on,” and other important feelings she cannot put into words are all mixed together. She calls that total bundle **her** honmei chocolate. card\_mei\_014\_st-card-mei-05-vln…

This is not standard romantic confession, but it is also not simple friendship. It is Mei naming her own category: a serious, special, future-oriented, emotionally dense gift that asks to be received seriously. The manager responds appropriately by not eating it casually; he says he cannot receive something made with Mei’s seriousness in a half-hearted way. Mei accepts that and asks him to tell her his feelings after eating it because she wants to properly know his feelings too. card\_mei\_014\_st-card-mei-05-vln…

**What it adds:**
Mei’s “love” is becoming more articulate. She still cannot sort it into conventional categories, but she now insists that the ambiguity itself is serious. Her honmei chocolate is not a mistake; it is her chosen word for a feeling larger than gratitude.

---

# Cross-card synthesis: Mei after this batch

## 1\. Mei’s central category is 本気, not maturity

The most important new word for Mei is **本気 / honki**: seriousness, genuine commitment, full-force feeling.

She is 本気 about underground-idol weirdness.
She is 本気 about helping Rei.
She is 本気 about giving Kotono a surprise.
She is 本気 about making her parents happy.
She is 本気 about Valentine’s chocolate.
She is 本気 about fans, friends, and “fun.”

This is why “mature” can be a misleading word for Mei. She does mature, but she does not mature by becoming calmer or more conventional. She matures by recognizing when her own chaotic energy contains a serious feeling.

## 2\. Mei’s care is action before explanation

Mei rarely begins with analysis. She acts.

Rei is not smiling while dancing → get her father to see the live.
Kotono has Christmas birthday → become Santa and make cake.
Father is lonely in hospital → take wedding-dress photo.
Manager struggles mixing cake → give him hand mixer.
Underground idol world is thrilling → participate and learn from it.
Valentine feelings are too many → put them all into chocolate.

This is why Mei can be both foolish and wise. She often chooses questionable methods, but the emotional direction is often correct.

## 3\. Mei’s relationship to the manager is now strongly “special,” but still category-unstable

This batch makes it impossible to reduce Mei’s manager relationship to simple father/daughter or staff/idol support.

She explicitly says “father” feels wrong. She notices his wet hair and feels dokidoki. She arranges a tuxedo-and-wedding-dress shoot. She says she wants him beside her forever. She gives him honmei chocolate. She asks to know his feelings. card\_mei\_016\_st-card-mei-05-wed… card\_mei\_014\_st-card-mei-05-vln…

At the same time, Mei’s emotional grammar remains broad. She immediately includes Moon Tempest, family, and cats in the same “fun to be with” category during the marriage discussion. Her honmei chocolate is not framed as a clean romantic confession but as a bundle of serious feelings: gratitude, desire to be watched, future promise, and something she cannot verbalize. card\_mei\_016\_st-card-mei-05-wed… card\_mei\_014\_st-card-mei-05-vln…

So the best formulation is:

**Mei’s feelings toward the manager are romantic-adjacent and deeply special, but not fully differentiated into adult romance. She is beginning to sense that some feelings are more serious than ordinary liking, even if she cannot name them cleanly.**

## 4\. Mei’s family warmth matters

Unlike some IDOLY PRIDE characters whose family relationships are sources of pressure, grief, or unresolved conflict, Mei’s family is a source of uncomplicated support and affection. Her parents support her idol work; her father’s loneliness moves her; she wants to cheer him up; she wants to send both parents on a trip. card\_mei\_016\_st-card-mei-05-wed… card\_mei\_017\_st-card-mei-05-xma…

This helps explain why Mei can give so freely. She comes from a relatively secure emotional base. She does not need to turn idolhood into proof of worth in the same way Rei does, or into legacy redemption like Kotono, or into relational self-definition like Nagisa. Mei’s idolhood grows out of abundance.

## 5\. Mei’s truthfulness is a performance asset

The Christmas card explicitly states that Mei’s words reach fans because there is no lie in them. This is one of the most important descriptions of her public idol appeal. card\_mei\_017\_st-card-mei-05-xma…

Mei is not polished in the Saki/Kotono sense, nor image-strategic like kana, nor narratively controlled like Nagisa. Her words work because they feel direct. She says what she means, often too quickly, often too much, but the audience can feel the sincerity.

## 6\. Mei can learn through any performance environment

Mei’s participation in underground idol battles is not just absurdity; it proves she is an experiential learner. She can learn from extreme venues, Rei’s dance, pair units, cats, food stands, wedding shoots, and fan reactions. card\_mei\_015\_st-card-mei-05-wdn… card\_mei\_013\_st-card-mei-05-roc…

Her learning style is wide-open. If an experience has energy, Mei absorbs it.

---

# Revised importance tiers

## Core

**面白いよね、『本命チョコ』**
Essential for Mei’s emotional maturation. It links center-battle frustration, 本気, special affection, and her inability-but-desire to know/communicate her own feelings. card\_mei\_014\_st-card-mei-05-vln…

**水が垂れてるいい男？**
Core for family warmth, wedding/ceremonial imagination, manager relationship ambiguity, and Mei’s future-oriented “ずっと一緒” language. card\_mei\_016\_st-card-mei-05-wed…

**街中すっかりクリスマス**
Core for Mei’s truthfulness, Kotono-birthday care, gift logic, and ability to be happy through giving rather than possession. card\_mei\_017\_st-card-mei-05-xma…

**楽しく踊ってる方がいい**
Core for Mei/Rei, Mei’s embodied relational intelligence, and her belief that performance should retain joy even when technically serious. card\_mei\_013\_st-card-mei-05-roc…

## Important

**どっかーーーーん**
Important for Mei as experiential idol, underground-performance openness, comic extremity, and her ability to remain an idol in strange spaces. It is less emotionally central than Valentine/wedding/Christmas/Rei, but very useful for understanding her range. card\_mei\_015\_st-card-mei-05-wdn…

---

# Updated Mei thesis after this batch

**Hayasaka Mei is Moon Tempest’s kinetic sun, but this batch reveals that her brightness is not mere cheer. It is full-force sincerity. Mei does not become serious by becoming solemn; she becomes serious by putting her whole self into whatever feeling has reached her — helping Rei dance happily, cheering up her father, surprising Kotono, making fans shout, protecting secrets without lying, joining strange stages, or giving a honmei chocolate whose contents she cannot fully name. Her emotional world remains broad and category-unstable, but that instability is part of her truth: gratitude, love, fun, rivalry, care, and desire all arrive in her as motion. Mei’s growth is learning to recognize those motions as real commitments, and to deliver them so honestly that other people cannot help but smile back.**

She remains Moon Tempest’s kinetic sun, but these messages and bond stories refine that into something more precise:

# Overall contribution to Mei

This batch strongly reinforces four traits.

First, Mei’s **voice** is now very clear. She speaks in bursts: “ねーねー,” stamps, sudden phone calls, exaggerated cries, sound effects, half-finished thoughts, and immediate emotional pivots. Her messages are not linear; they are kinetic. A conversation about schoolwork becomes donuts, then cats, then a life hack, then a declaration of genius. bond\_mei\_001\_mei message\_message\_group\_mei\_001\_m…

Second, Mei’s care is often **clumsy but sincere**. She breaks bowls, eats the wrong food, drags people into trouble, forgets her bento, causes Saki stress, and tries to climb walls after cats. But when she understands why someone was worried, she responds with apology, gratitude, or an attempt to change. bond\_mei\_001\_mei message\_message\_group\_mei\_001\_m…

Third, her manager relationship becomes more obviously **emotionally special**. She wants to talk, wants to see his face, wants him to come to her jobs, wants to know more about him, has dreams of dating him, frames small eye contact as a secret, and treats “two-person secrets” as special proof of closeness. This does not erase her category-instability around love, but it makes the specialness much harder to dismiss. message\_message\_group\_mei\_001\_m… message\_message\_group\_mei\_001\_m… message\_message\_group\_mei\_001\_m…

Fourth, the batch clarifies Mei’s actual professional growth: she is learning that her spontaneous ideas need **consultation, timing, technique, and adaptation**. Her handshake-talk problem, dance-arrangement arc, and fan-observation messages all show Mei converting instinct into skill. message\_message\_group\_mei\_001\_m… message\_message\_group\_mei\_001\_m…

---

# 1\. Bond stories: Mei’s everyday system

**Importance tier: Core texture.**

The bond stories are invaluable because they show Mei in ordinary life: lunch, donuts, cats, dishes, math class, selfie filters, yoga, and late-night “date?” confusion. These do not redefine her main arc, but they make her feel fully alive.

The first bond story, where Mei forgets her bento and Kotono shares lunch, establishes a key everyday dynamic: Mei is careless but not malicious, and Kotono often becomes the straight man who protects her from small disasters. Mei’s proposed solution — attach her lunch box to her phone like a keychain — is absurd, but it shows her problem-solving style: she does not think “be more disciplined”; she thinks “make the forgotten thing physically impossible to lose.” bond\_mei\_001\_mei

The donut rock-paper-scissors story with Kotono is even better. Mei tries to help Kotono avoid calorie guilt by turning donut consumption into a game of luck. It is a very Mei solution: transform restraint into play. But because Mei keeps losing, Kotono ends up eating far more than intended, and Mei keeps demanding rematches. The point is not only food comedy. It shows Mei’s belief that rules, games, and shared fun can make awkward desires easier to accept. bond\_mei\_001\_mei

The roadside “cat café” story is especially important. Mei creates 猫カフェ『めいきゃっと』 out of a vending-machine drink and stray cats. This is one of the cleanest images of Mei’s imagination: she does not need an official venue to create an experience. If there are cats, a friend, a drink, and shared attention, she can make a “café.” Kotono’s unexpectedly deep cat fascination also lets Mei become the guide rather than the childlike one for once. bond\_mei\_001\_mei

The bowl-breaking story shows Mei’s care becoming more person-specific. At first she wants to buy a cat bowl because she likes cats. Kotono reminds her that the bowl is for Saki. Mei eventually identifies a cat design tied to Saki’s preferred character, showing that her empathy can sharpen when guided. But the later Rio bowl accident also shows the limitation: she overgeneralizes a successful solution. What worked for Saki will not automatically work for everyone. bond\_mei\_001\_mei

The math-class story is perfect Mei voice: probability problem → Saki tutoring → donut reward → limited donut release → bento reminder system → feeding local cat → accidental correct answer. Her mind wanders constantly, but not into emptiness. It wanders into a dense associative network of food, friends, habits, cats, and small life systems. bond\_mei\_001\_mei

The selfie-app story reinforces Mei’s supernatural register. Saki sees a filter react to empty space and panics; Mei treats it casually and ends with “you got in the picture too,” implying that for Mei, the invisible presence is not shocking. This is not just a gag. Mei is written as porous to cats, ghosts, future selves, and odd phenomena in a way that fits her boundary-loose emotional world. bond\_mei\_001\_mei

The yoga and Kokoro stories deepen her comic physicality. Mei wakes people with “シムハーサナ” because she wants to get hungry before a cake buffet, and she later borrows a dryer because she wants to look nice before waiting in line at a donut shop with the manager — then has to consider whether that counts as a date. This is exactly Mei: bodily, food-motivated, romance-curious, and only halfway aware of the implications. bond\_mei\_001\_mei

**What it adds:**
The bond stories make Mei’s daily self coherent: forgetful, tactile, appetite-driven, animal-oriented, supernatural-adjacent, socially porous, and constantly turning ordinary life into little events.

---

# 2\. Message voice: Mei as burst communication

**Importance tier: Core for voice.**

The message corpus is one of the best sources for writing Mei accurately.

Mei’s message style is not polished. It is made of:

-   repeated calls for attention;

-   sudden stamps;

-   abrupt phone requests;

-   elongated words;

-   sound effects;

-   overexcited declarations;

-   unfinished thought chains;

-   emotional whiplash;

-   and topic switches that make sense only inside Mei’s associative logic.


She frequently opens with “Manager-san!” or stamps, then immediately leaps into a crisis, idea, invitation, or emotional report. She often decides a phone call would be faster. She is not using messaging as a formal communication channel; she is using it as a live extension of her body. message\_message\_group\_mei\_001\_m… message\_message\_group\_mei\_001\_m…

A typical Mei message rhythm is:

1.  Emergency-style opening.

2.  Odd statement.

3.  Manager asks for clarification.

4.  Mei explains by association.

5.  The explanation creates a new tangent.

6.  She asks to call, visit, eat, play, or make a plan.


This rhythm matters because it distinguishes her from Nagisa. Nagisa’s messages overflow through emotional narration and apology spirals. Mei’s overflow is more physical and impulsive: **do the thing, call now, go now, eat now, show now, try now.**

---

# 3\. Mei’s manager relationship: special, needy, playful, category-unstable

This batch strengthens the reading that Mei’s relationship with the manager is emotionally special in a way she cannot fully categorize.

She wants to go to the countryside with him alone because the game version of Mei did so with the protagonist. She wants him to accompany her to a donut shop before opening and wonders whether that counts as a date. She says she feels happiest when he smiles while alone with her. She gets lonely when he does not come to her jobs. She wants to see his face, wants to know more about him, and wants secrets only she knows. She dreams about dating him and says she will wait for him in another dream. message\_message\_group\_mei\_001\_m… message\_message\_group\_mei\_001\_m… message\_message\_group\_mei\_001\_m…

The important nuance is that Mei’s desire for closeness is not always self-consciously romantic. She often frames it as fun, talking, being together, secrets, shared food, or wanting him to watch her. But the accumulation is clearly romantic-adjacent.

The phrase “two-person secret” recurs as a major intimacy marker. Mei treats secrets not as deception but as evidence of closeness. In the Valentine follow-up, she specifically says the honmei chocolate is a secret between the two of them. In another message, even a moment of eye contact from the wings becomes “二人だけの秘密だね,” and she is happy about it. message\_message\_group\_mei\_001\_m… message\_message\_group\_mei\_001\_m…

This is different from Nagisa’s romance-coded manager scenes. Nagisa often understands romance as genre and then gets embarrassed. Mei experiences closeness first, then later asks whether the scene might be a date. She does not start with the trope; she backs into it through feeling.

**Updated formulation:**
Mei’s manager feelings are **special attachment before romantic literacy**. She wants more shared time, more private knowledge, more affirmation, more secrets, and more mutual recognition. Whether she can label that as romance is secondary; the intensity is real.

---

# 4\. Mei’s learning style: from impulse to technique

A major contribution of the message arcs is showing Mei becoming more professional without becoming less Mei.

The dance-arrangement arc is especially clear. At first, Mei spontaneously changes choreography and gets scolded because unilateral improvisation disrupts the group. The manager tells her to consult first. She then learns: talk first, then act. Later, her dance idea is accepted, and she proudly reports that the live was exciting because of the movement she proposed. Crucially, she also credits the members for executing it well. message\_message\_group\_mei\_001\_m… message\_message\_group\_mei\_001\_m…

That is Mei’s growth in miniature:

**Impulse → correction → consultation → adoption → shared success.**

The handshake-time arc is also important. Mei struggles because fan conversations end before she finishes. She compares interrupted fan talk to having a donut taken away mid-eating — a silly metaphor, but emotionally exact for her. She tries timers, fails because her own voice is too loud, then learns from other members to measure by topics or by her own speaking rhythm. This is not merely comedy. It shows Mei learning fan-service technique in a way suited to her sensory style. message\_message\_group\_mei\_001\_m… message\_message\_group\_mei\_001\_m…

The fan-observation messages reinforce the same development. Mei watches Rui and Yu talk to fans, notices they return questions and make eye contact, and decides to learn from them. Her first instinct is not always technically refined, but she is teachable and observational when she has a concrete model. message\_message\_group\_mei\_001\_m…

**What it adds:**
Mei’s professional growth is not about suppressing spontaneity. It is about learning channels for it.

---

# 5\. Mei’s self-image: “am I really an idol?”

Under the obvious cheerfulness, Mei has more insecurity than she first appears to have.

She wonders if she is properly shining after seeing Kotono and Sakura. She asks whether she is truly doing idol work. She wants a “special ability” because idols should be admired, and she sees Kotono’s polish and Rei’s dance as more obviously aspirational. When a fan says Mei feels like a friend and is easy to approach, she finally recognizes that this too can be a “special ability.” message\_message\_group\_mei\_001\_m… message\_message\_group\_mei\_001\_m…

This is crucial because it shows Mei’s insecurity is not “I am worthless” in a melodramatic sense. It is more innocent and comparative:

**If idols are supposed to be admired from above, is my friendliness enough?**

The answer the text gives is yes. Mei’s “friend-like” quality is not a failure to be idol-like; it is her idol type. She is not the distant star, the perfect leader, the cool dancer, or the romantic scenarist. She is the idol who feels close enough that fans want to smile back.

This connects perfectly with the earlier “donut-type idol” idea. Mei’s public appeal is circular, not vertical. She does not only ask fans to look up at her; she makes them feel like they are inside the ring.

---

# 6\. Mana, aspiration, and surpassing

The Mana dream messages are small but very important. Mei wonders whether wanting to become like Mana is really what Mana would want, especially when considering Kotono and Sakura. The manager suggests that Mana would want her to surpass her, and later Mei dreams that Mana tells her to become an idol who shines more than Mana. message\_message\_group\_mei\_001\_m…

This is a key refinement of Mei’s relationship to Mana.

Mei’s Mana relationship is not grief-bound like Kotono’s. It is not purely reverential like Suzu’s. Mei treats Mana as inspiration, but she is capable of asking whether imitation is the wrong answer. The dream resolves it in a very Mei-compatible way: do not become Mana; shine more.

This gives Mei a healthy ambition structure. She can admire Mana without being trapped by her. The goal is not imitation, but joyful escalation.

---

# 7\. Mei and Moon Tempest: troublemaker, social glue, and corrective learner

The bond/messages show Mei’s relationship with Moon Tempest as a constant mixture of chaos and affection.

With Kotono, Mei is loud, food-driven, and often exasperating, but she also brings Kotono into playful spaces: shared lunch, donuts, fake cat cafés. Kotono often corrects Mei, but also indulges her more than she would admit. bond\_mei\_001\_mei

With Saki, Mei repeatedly causes discipline problems — chasing Suzu, breaking bowls, ignoring safety talks, rearranging choreography, climbing after cats — but she also learns from Saki’s concern. The sea-safety message is especially important because Mei realizes that Saki’s anger came from fear rooted in Chisa nearly drowning. Mei decides to thank Saki rather than just apologize. That is a mature emotional adjustment. message\_message\_group\_mei\_001\_m…

With Suzu, Mei forms the clearest chaos duo. She plays, chases, shares snacks, sends photos so the manager can praise Suzu, asks the manager to imitate Suzu when Suzu’s phone is unavailable, and repeatedly treats Suzu as someone whose efforts deserve recognition. message\_message\_group\_mei\_001\_m… message\_message\_group\_mei\_001\_m…

With the group as a whole, Mei is often the person who accidentally creates trouble but also keeps relational warmth moving. She is not the unit’s stable center. She is the live wire that keeps the system from becoming too stiff.

---

# 8\. Mei’s ethics: apology, gratitude, and repair

Mei is not careless because she lacks conscience. The messages repeatedly show that once she understands the harm or worry she caused, she wants to repair it.

She apologizes to Saki after chasing Suzu in the dorm. She reports eating staff bento and acknowledges Rui and Saki scolded her. She worries that her sea behavior frightened Saki and decides to thank her properly. She washes the manager’s slippers after spilling coffee on them. She limits herself from climbing after cats when she realizes Saki gave her a cat photo book out of concern. message\_message\_group\_mei\_001\_m… message\_message\_group\_mei\_001\_m… message\_message\_group\_mei\_001\_m…

This gives Mei a very specific moral profile:

**She is impulsive before the fact, but repair-oriented after the fact.**

That repair orientation keeps her chaos from becoming selfishness. She causes trouble, but she is not indifferent to the people affected by it.

---

# 9\. Food, cats, and body: not gags but organizing principles

The body is central to Mei.

Donuts, karaage, cake, bento, limited sweets, staff lunches, yoga to get hungry, calorie anxiety, and walking with Haruko all show that Mei thinks through appetite. Food is not just something she likes; it is how she structures reward, apology, memory, celebration, temptation, and sharing. bond\_mei\_001\_mei message\_message\_group\_mei\_001\_m… message\_message\_group\_mei\_001\_m…

Cats are equally central. Mei uses cats as companions, metaphors, work motifs, filters, gifts, risk objects, and bridges to new friendships. The cat-allergy arc is particularly revealing: when a new friend loves cats but cannot physically play with them, Mei creates a substitute experience with a stuffed cat she animates by hand. It is silly, but the emotional structure is real: Mei wants to give someone the experience they long for, even if she has to improvise a childish workaround. message\_message\_group\_mei\_001\_m…

The “ぽっこりしない理由” arc also clarifies Mei’s body. She eats freely but remains slim not because of magic dieting, but because she is constantly moving. Haruko cannot keep up when imitating her day. Mei’s body is not decorative; it is the engine of her personality. message\_message\_group\_mei\_001\_m…

---

# 10\. Mei’s relationship to time and memory

Mei often lives in the immediate now, but this batch shows she does care about time passing.

She notices that fun time with fans ends too quickly. She wants conversations to last. She is sad when work separates her from the manager. She wants shared moments to repeat: countryside trips, donut outings, cake-making, phone calls, photos, secrets. Birthday messages repeatedly show that celebration makes her chest warm, but she eventually realizes daily life with Hoshimi and the manager is already joyful. message\_message\_group\_mei\_001\_m… message\_message\_group\_mei\_001\_m… message\_message\_group\_mei\_001\_m…

Mei is less archival than Nagisa; she does not preserve memory through diaries or romantic scripts. She preserves memory through **repetition and reenactment**: do it again, go again, call again, eat together again, make another cake, have another secret, see each other tomorrow.

That is a valuable contrast:

-   Nagisa turns memory into scenes.

-   Kotono turns memory into responsibility.

-   Mei turns memory into “let’s do it again.”


---

# Importance tiers

## Core for Mei

**Bond stories: Mei**
Core for everyday characterization: forgetfulness, food, Kotono dynamic, cat-space creation, Saki/Rio bowl mistakes, supernatural casualness, yoga absurdity, and “dateかも?” innocence. bond\_mei\_001\_mei

**Mana dream messages**
Core for Mei’s aspiration. She moves from wanting to become like Mana to accepting that Mana would want her to shine beyond Mana. message\_message\_group\_mei\_001\_m…

**Special ability messages**
Core for Mei’s self-image. She learns that being friend-like and approachable is not a failure of idolhood but her own “special ability.” message\_message\_group\_mei\_001\_m…

**Dance arrangement messages**

# Updated Mei model after bonds/messages

At this point, Mei can be described across seven axes.

## 1\. Core temperament

Mei is immediate, physical, affectionate, appetite-driven, and sensory. She experiences the world as invitations: eat, run, play, call, sing, try, chase, hug, ask, repeat.

## 2\. Core strength

Her strength is **contact**. She can make people feel included quickly because she approaches without calculation. Fans, children, cats, elders, classmates, staff, and idols all become part of her circle.

## 3\. Core flaw

Her flaw is **unmediated impulse**. She acts before checking safety, rules, timing, or consent. But she is unusually repairable: once corrected, she often understands and tries again.

## 4\. Idol weapon

Her idol weapon is approachable brightness. Fans experience her as friend-like, energetic, sincere, and easy to love. This is not inferior to cool charisma; it is her own form of aspiration.

## 5\. Professional growth

She is learning to convert instinct into method: consult before arranging choreography, time fan conversations by rhythm, observe how seniors talk to fans, and use her natural voice without letting it overwhelm structure.

## 6\. Emotional categories

Her love categories remain broad. Friendship, romance, family, fan gratitude, food-sharing, group loyalty, secrets, and “fun together” all overlap. This makes her emotionally ambiguous, but not shallow. Her feelings are real before they are named.

## 7\. Relationship to the manager

The manager is a stabilizing anchor, but also a special object of curiosity, comfort, and possible pre-romantic attachment. Mei wants his attention and presence not only as staff support but as a personal source of energy.

---

# Final updated Mei thesis

**Hayasaka Mei is Moon Tempest’s kinetic sun and affection-first experiential learner: a girl whose apparent randomness is actually a consistent way of turning feeling into action. She forgets lunches, chases cats, breaks bowls, overtalks, overplays, invents strange concepts, and calls people at odd moments, but underneath that chaos is a sincere desire to connect, repair, share, and make joy repeatable. Her growth is not becoming calm or conventional. It is learning how to consult before acting, pace fan conversations, recognize worry beneath scolding, accept that her approachability is an idol strength, and put her full-force sincerity into forms other people can safely receive.**

**Mei’s charm is that she does not shine above people. She shines into them. She makes the world feel like a place where even a roadside cat gathering can become a café, a forgotten lunch can become shared karaage, a stuffed cat can comfort an allergic friend, a donut can become a philosophy of connection, and an ordinary message can become a reason to call, laugh, and keep moving.**

# Overall contribution to Mei

Across these events, Mei gains five major dimensions.

First, **she is more central to Moon Tempest’s creative identity than her comic surface suggests**. In 「月夜に輝く恋の魔法」, she is the one who insists that Moon Tempest needs a summer song, then discovers the concept that makes “Moon Tempest’s summer” distinct from Sunny Peace’s sunlit image: night, fireworks, confession, moonlight, and the beauty that appears because it is not daylight. IMPORTANT\_002\_\_event\_2021\_002\_s…

Second, **her relationship with Suzu becomes one of her defining growth engines**. 「迷走ピリオド 涼やかな青春」 shows that Mei can be hurt, jealous, confused, and angry — not in an abstract internal monologue, but through her fear that Suzu will not hear her. It is one of the clearest stories proving that Mei’s kindness is not effortless innocence; sometimes she has to learn to fight honestly. IMPORTANT\_015\_\_event\_2023\_010\_s…

Third, **her ambition becomes explicit**. In 「ぶつかり愛のCenter Battle！」, Mei does not merely enter the center competition for fun. She works so hard she cuts into donut time, declares she is serious, and later breaks down because she truly wanted to be center. CORE\_009\_\_event\_2026\_002\_st-eve… +1

Fourth, **Mana becomes a positive aspirational model rather than a confusing object of imitation**. Mei repeatedly connects her ideal to Mana: making people happy, shining beyond Mana, learning from Mana’s kindness, and remembering Mana as someone who could laugh, shop, fight, overwork, and be corrected. This is different from Kotono’s wound or Suzu’s worship. Mei’s Mana is a remembered person who teaches how to live with full feeling. IMPORTANT\_015\_\_event\_2023\_010\_s…

Fifth, **Mei’s idea of idolhood expands beyond the official VENUS framework**. The underground arena event is comic, but it matters: Mei can recognize idol sincerity even in absurd, non-sanctioned, low-prestige spaces. She learns that technical excellence is not the whole of idol value; sometimes the better idol is the one who makes the audience want to cheer. IMPORTANT\_026\_\_event\_2026\_004\_s…
